We need to prove a parametricity theorem for System F with nondeterminism. We must construct a logical relation. The target is: for any closed term t : ∀X. X → X, and closed well-formed type U, value v with has_type [] empty v U, we have `tm_app (tm_tapp t U) v -->* v`.

Note important: the conclusion is a specific reduction to v, and with nondeterminism term t may involve choice. However the "free theorem" here says that any term of that type, applied, reduces to v along some path (multi). That's a strong result. Let's think.

Actually, a term of type ∀X. X → X at base type must be either identity-ish; with nondeterminism, choice allows two branches both of same type. E.g. t = Lambda, choice (lambda x. x) (lambda x. x). Both behave as identity.

We need a logical relation to prove this. Let's think about the semantics of the language.

The language: types:
- Ty_BVar for de Bruijn indices in type abstraction (used in T_App with `open_ty T U`).
- Ty_FVar for free type variables (context Delta).
- Arrow, All.

Terms: tm_bvar de Bruijn for term variables (bound by tm_abs), tm_fvar free variables, abs, app, tabs, tapp, choice. Note: tm_tabs does not bind term variables, and tm_abs does not bind type variables. Type abstraction is done via `open_tm_ty t (Ty_FVar X)` in T_TAbs: body uses Ty_FVar X after opening. Wait open_tm_ty replaces Ty_BVar indices with U, but how does tm_tabs's body reference the bound type variable? It would use Ty_BVar 0. T_TAbs says has_type (X :: Delta) Gamma (open_tm_ty t (Ty_FVar X)) (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)). And T_TApp says type of tm_tapp t U is open_ty T U. So t's type is Ty_All T where T has bound var 0 referencing the type parameter, and the term body inside tm_tabs uses Ty_BVar 0 within type annotations.

Hmm, note wf_ty Delta T with Delta a list of atoms (free type vars). For Ty_All T, WF_All requires for fresh X, wf_ty (X::Delta) (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)). So no de Bruijn in wf types after All; they get opened to free vars.

Value relation semantics: We might define a logical relation on terms at closed types? The theorem involves U with wf_ty [] U, so U is a closed type (but might contain Ty_BVar? wf_ty [] (Ty_BVar i) is not constructible because only WF_Var for Ty_FVar, WF_Arrow, WF_All. So no Ty_BVar at top-level. But under All, open_ty yields FVars.)

We need relation for types with free type vars possibly (Delta). The standard: semantic interpretation of type T in type context Delta and "type relation" environment rho mapping each X in Delta to a binary relation on closed terms (or on terms of a certain type). Then logical relation V[[T]]rho relates terms t1 t2 (binary). Then the fundamental theorem: if has_type Delta Gamma t T, then for any two related term environments, related. Then the free theorem follows by taking t1 = t2 = t? Hmm.

For the identity theorem, we want: t : ∀X. X→X. Interpret type ∀X. X→X as: for all closed U, related... Standard parametricity: a term of type ∀X. X→X satisfies that for all types U, the instantiation is related to itself at relation. To get the conclusion t U v -->* v, we need the relation to be such that relatedness at U of (t U) and identity implies application reduces to v.

Alternative simpler approach: define a "semantic" relation R_T on values/terms with the property: if t : T and t is related to itself, then ... The classic "identity extension" gives: for closed U, t U is related to itself at U→U. The relation at U→U says: for all v,v' related at U, applications are related at U. With identity relation at U (v related to v), we get t U v related to itself at U. Then we need a lemma: any closed term of a "closed" type U related to itself evaluates (multi-step) to... hmm, not exactly a value; we need it to evaluate to v itself when applied to v. Hmm.

Let's think: We want `tm_app (tm_tapp t U) v -->* v` for any value v of type U. This is exactly strong: the term acts as identity on any value, not just related pair. Key point: a term of type ∀X.X→X is parametric; applied to U and v, it must return v. With nondeterminism, it might choose among paths but there exists a path to v.

Approach: define logical relation with the "reduction to" flavor. Perhaps interpret types as relations on terms where relatedness at T implies some evaluation property. Standard technique: logical relations for parametricity with a "relational" interpretation, plus the identity extension lemma (for closed types, relation at T with all-identity environment equals equality/self-relatedness). Then to show t U v reduces to v, we do:

Now, what exactly is a "relation"? Since types may be interpreted at terms, but the free type variable X : Delta can be instantiated with arbitrary closed types; however the logical relation for FVar X should be a relation between terms (of possibly different types U1, U2). Since we're doing a binary logical relation intended for proving the free theorem, we can define relation as pairs of terms (tm -> tm -> Prop) without type indices. For arrow T1 -> T2: relation between functions f1,f2 such that for all a1,a2 related at T1, f1 a1 related to f2 a2 at T2? This works if relations on terms are untyped. But then the fundamental theorem: if t1 has type T under env1 and t2 under env2, and env1,env2 pointwise related, then t1,t2 related at T.

However, the arrow case relation should be defined appropriately so that the parametricity interpretation is sound. Let's design the semantics carefully to make the proof work with nondeterminism.

Let's define the interpretation of types as relations on terms, parameterized by:
- `l : list (tm -> tm -> Prop)` for de Bruijn indices (innermost first)
- `rho : atom -> tm -> tm -> Prop` for free type vars.

Definition:
```
Fixpoint rel_ty (T : ty) (l : list (tm -> tm -> Prop)) (rho : atom -> tm -> tm -> Prop) : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => nth i l (fun _ _ => False)
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun t1 t2 => forall a1 a2, rel_ty T1 l rho a1 a2 -> rel_ty T2 l rho (tm_app t1 a1) (tm_app t2 a2)
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      fun t1 t2 => forall (R : tm -> tm -> Prop) (U1 U2 : ty),
         rel_ty T1 (R :: l) rho (tm_tapp t1 U1) (tm_tapp t2 U2)
  end.
```

Hold on. Maybe we can strengthen the fundamental theorem to directly handle arbitrary type substitutions by including them in the statement, as I considered. Let's formulate:

Define `tysubst := atom -> ty` (total or partial; use total with default arbitrary). A *type environment* `sigma : atom -> ty` gives types for FVars. A relation environment `rho : atom -> (tm -> tm -> Prop)` gives relations. For all Delta Gamma t T,
has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
forall sigma1 sigma2 rho eta1 eta2,
  (term env relation: forall y S, In (y,S) Gamma -> erel (S with sigma?) ... eta1 y eta2 y) ->
  rel_ty T [] rho sigma1 sigma2 (subst_tm sigma1 eta1 t) (subst_tm sigma2 eta2 t).

Where rel_ty T bl rho sigma1 sigma2 is indexed by two type substitutions to interpret types containing BVars? Hmm, the type T's free FVars are Delta; BVars in T refer to Alls in T (since T is wf in Delta). So T itself can be interpreted with bl from its own Alls and rho for Delta; no sigma needed. But the *terms* being related are instantiated with sigma1/sigma2 (type substitution of free type vars). The relation at type T is the same for both terms. So the statement is:

has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
forall sigma1 sigma2 rho eta1 eta2,
  (forall y S, In (y,S) Gamma -> erel S rho sigma1 sigma2? (eta1 y) (eta2 y)) ->
  rel_ty T [] rho (subst_tm_ty_env sigma1 eta1 ...) ...

But the context Gamma's types S contain FVars from Delta; their interpretation uses rho. Term env terms eta1 y, eta2 y are terms from the outside; they may contain type annotations of their own (already instantiated?). In T_Abs, the body premise has Gamma' = (x,T1)::Gamma; the IH for the body with term env extended by actual arguments a1,a2: those arguments are terms of the outer context, whose types S maybe after sigma? Hmm, the IH's env relation requires erel S rho ... (eta1 x) (eta2 x). eta1 x = a1. If a1 is a value related at S, okay. So the fundamental theorem statement:

```
Theorem fundamental :
  forall Delta Gamma t T,
    has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
    forall (bl : list (tm -> tm -> Prop)) (rho : atom -> tm -> tm -> Prop)
           (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> ty) (eta1 eta2 : atom -> tm),
      (forall (y : atom) (S : ty), In (y,S) Gamma ->
         erel S bl rho (eta1 y) (eta2 y)) ->
      rel_ty T bl rho (subst_tm_type sigma1 eta1 t) (subst_tm_type sigma2 eta2 t).
```

Wait, but what is `subst_tm_type sigma eta t`? It substitutes free term vars y with eta y (term substitution) and free type vars X in annotations with sigma X (type substitution), and also does opening? The term t from the typing derivation may contain tm_fvar and type annotations with FVars. But T_Abs/T_TAbs premises use `open_tm`/`open_tm_ty` with fresh FVar x / FVar X, which are syntactically present as FVars in the premise term. In the fundamental theorem, to get the relation for the *original* abs term's body applied to actual argument a, we go from the premise term `open_tm t2 (FVar x)` to substitute x:=a via tm_subst and instantiate type vars. The typing premise's term contains FVar x; the term env maps x to a1. Substituting term vars via eta will replace FVar x with a1. Good. The type substitution sigma handles type FVars. So `subst_tm_type sigma eta t` = substitute free term variables according to eta and free type variables according to sigma in t.

But note: the lambda body in the typing premise is `open_tm t2 (FVar x)`; the original body is t2 (with BVars). In the T_Abs case of the fundamental theorem, the conclusion term is `tm_abs T1 t2` (with free type vars); substituting type vars gives `tm_abs (T1[sigma]) (t2[sigma])`; the term relation at arrow requires app (abs T1[sigma1] t2[sigma1]) a1. But for the desired All semantics, we're free to choose the interpretation of the body under binder as R0 at BVar0, *regardless* of the type U used for instantiation. This is exactly the "type erasure" of the logical relation: the relation ignores type annotations, and instantiating the polymorphic term at U1 vs U2 just changes annotations but the logical relation at BVar0 is R0. So the fundamental theorem we want is about *term behavior* under type substitution, and the type annotation substitution is semantically irrelevant. To bridge, we need a lemma on logical relations under type substitution in annotations: if t1 ~ t2 at type T under env where FVar X ↦ R0, then (type-substituted t1) ~ (type-substituted t2) at the same "T with X replacing" relation. This is essentially a type substitution lemma for the logical relation, which we can prove by induction on T if we define the relation in a type-erased way.

- T = BVar i: open_ty_rec k (FVar X) (BVar i) = if k =? i then FVar X else BVar i.

Let's define rel_ty with `bl : list (tm -> tm -> Prop)` where bl[0] is the innermost binder. Recursion on type:
- All T1 => relation = fun t1 t2 => forall R, rel_ty T1 (R :: bl) rho ... (if the relation at All is defined on tapp terms? We'll get to that.)
- BVar i => nth i bl.

Now open_ty_rec k U T replaces BVar k in T by U. Under an All inside T, k becomes S k in the recursive call. The semantic lemma at the top (for a type T interpreted with bl = the outer env):
  rel_ty (open_ty_rec k (FVar X) T) bl rho' = rel_ty T (insert_at k R0 bl) rho,
where insert_at k R0 bl = first k elements of bl, then R0, then rest. Check BVar i at top depth:
- i = k: LHS R0 (FVar X interpreted by rho'), RHS nth k (insert_at k R0 bl) = R0 ✓.
- i < k: LHS nth i bl, RHS nth i insert = nth i bl ✓.
- i > k: LHS nth i bl (open leaves it); RHS nth i insert = nth (i-1) bl ✗ unless... So the lemma requires that in T, no BVar index > k occurs at a depth where it's interpreted with bl? But as argued, in well-scoped T under k binders (bl length k, i.e., indices 0..k-1 bound), any occurrence of BVar i must have i < k + d where d is internal depth, and at depth d open_ty has k+d. So at the top (d=0), i<k, so i>k impossible. Under All, we recurse with k'=k+1 and bl'=R::bl; at that point valid i < k'+d' etc. So the "i>k" case at any recursion depth where T is well-scoped doesn't occur. But in Coq, our induction on ty will hit the i>k case abstractly; we need a hypothesis excluding it, or define the semantics to be robust so the lemma holds even for i>k. Can we define insert_at differently to make it hold for all i? We want nth i (insert_at k R0 bl) = nth i bl for i≠k. That's impossible with a list insertion shifting. Unless bl is infinite / a function. Use a *function* environment instead of a list: `benv : nat -> tm -> tm -> Prop`. Then the lemma's RHS: rel_ty T (fun i => if i =? k then R0 else benv i) ... and All case pushes by shifting: benv' i = benv (i+1)? Let's define:

rel_ty T benv rho where benv : nat -> (tm -> tm -> Prop).
- BVar i => benv i
- All T1 => forall R, rel_ty T1 (fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => benv j end) rho
- Arrow => ...

Then open_ty_rec k (FVar X) T: semantic lemma:
`rel_ty (open_ty_rec k (FVar X) T) benv rho' = rel_ty T (fun i => if i =? k then R0 else benv i) rho`
Check BVar i:
- i=k: LHS R0, RHS R0 ✓
- else: LHS benv i, RHS benv i ✓.
Under All: LHS rel_ty (All (open_ty_rec (S k) (FVar X) T1)) benv rho' = forall R, rel_ty (open_ty_rec (S k) (FVar X) T1) (shift benv R) rho'. IH with k:=S k and benv:=shift benv R: RHS = rel_ty T1 (fun i => if i =? S k then R0 else shift benv R i) rho. RHS of overall: rel_ty (All T1) (fun i => if i =? k then R0 else benv i) rho = forall R, rel_ty T1 (shift (fun i => if i=?k then R0 else benv i) R) rho = forall R, rel_ty T1 (fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => (if j =? k then R0 else benv j) end) rho.
Compare with (fun i => if i =? S k then R0 else (shift benv R) i):
- i=0: R.
- i=S j: if S j =? S k then R0 else shift benv R (S j) = if j =? k then R0 else benv j.
Same! Great. So function-based de Bruijn env makes the lemma work without well-scopedness conditions. 

So define benv : nat -> (tm -> tm -> Prop). The All case shifts.

### Definitions

Types with de Bruijn env as function:
```
Definition Rel := tm -> tm -> Prop.
Definition ty_env := nat -> Rel.     (* bound type vars, de Bruijn *)
Definition free_ty_env := atom -> Rel.  (* free type vars *)
Definition value_rel (R : Rel) : Prop := forall t1 t2, R t1 t2 -> value t1 /\ value t2.
```

Interpretation of types:
```
Fixpoint rel_ty (T : ty) (benv : ty_env) (rho : free_ty_env) : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => benv i
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun t1 t2 =>
        value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall a1 a2, rel_ty T1 benv rho a1 a2 ->
                      erel T2 benv rho (tm_app t1 a1) (tm_app t2 a2)
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      fun t1 t2 =>
        value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall (U1 U2 : ty) (R : Rel),
          locally_closed_ty U1 -> locally_closed_ty U2 ->
          value_rel R ->
          erel T1 (fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => benv j end) rho
               (tm_tapp t1 U1) (tm_tapp t2 U2)
  end
with erel (T : ty) (benv : ty_env) (rho : free_ty_env) : Rel :=
  fun t1 t2 => exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_ty T benv rho v1 v2
```

For the arrow relation, the value conjuncts might be redundant if rho is a value relation and the type structure is built from value relations. But keep them for convenience.

We need a notion of substituting free type variables according to sigma and free term vars according to eta into a term. Define:
```
Fixpoint subst_ty_all (sigma : atom -> ty) (t : tm) : tm := ...  (* like tm_ty_subst but for all atoms *)
Fixpoint subst_tm_all (eta : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ... (* like tm_subst but for all atoms *)
Definition inst_tm (sigma : atom -> ty) (eta : atom -> tm) (t : tm) := subst_tm_all eta (subst_ty_all sigma t).
```
Or define a simultaneous substitution. Simpler to reuse tm_ty_subst with a function. Let's define:

```
Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env (sigma : atom -> ty) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (ty_subst_env sigma T)
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  end
with ty_subst_env sigma T := ...
```

Let's see if we can prove the semantic lemma without extensionality by choosing the environment representation so that the equality is definitional. Use a "cons-list-like" function with explicit shift defined so that the two sides are convertible under `simpl`/`nat` pattern matching. The issue: `Nat.eqb` doesn't reduce on variables. We can define the environment `benv : nat -> Rel` and a push operation `push (R : Rel) (benv : nat -> Rel) : nat -> Rel := fun i => match i with 0 => R | S j => benv j end`. Then `push R benv 0 = R` and `push R benv (S j) = benv j` are definitional. The semantic lemma can be stated with a `push_at k R benv` operation defined recursively on k:

```
Fixpoint push_at (k : nat) (R : Rel) (benv : nat -> Rel) : nat -> Rel :=
  match k with
  | 0 => push R benv
  | S k' => fun i => match i with
                     | 0 => benv 0
                     | S j => push_at k' R (fun i => benv (S i)) j
                     end
  end.
```

Define:
```
Fixpoint rel_ty (d : nat) (T : ty) (benv : nat -> Rel) (rho : free_env) : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i =>
      (* at depth d, BVar i refers to the binder at level d - 1 - i if i < d? *)
      benv (d - 1 - i)
  ...
  | Ty_All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value... ∧ forall R, erel (S d) T1 (add_benv d R benv) rho (tapp...)
```

Let's write:
```
Fixpoint rel_ty (T : ty) (d : nat) (benv : nat -> Rel) (rho : atom -> Rel) : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => benv (d - 1 - i)
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun t1 t2 =>
        value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall a1 a2, rel_ty T1 d benv rho a1 a2 ->
          (exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\
                         rel_ty T2 d benv rho v1 v2)
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      fun t1 t2 =>
        value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall (U1 U2 : ty) (R : Rel),
          locally_closed_ty U1 -> locally_closed_ty U2 ->
          value_rel R ->
          (exists w1 w2,
             tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tm_tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\
             rel_ty T1 (S d) (fun j => if j =? d then R else benv j) rho w1 w2)
  end.
```
Then `Definition erel T rho t1 t2 := exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_ty T ... v1 v2`.

But rel_ty is supposed to be a *value relation*; when used in erel, it's applied to values. Including value makes it easy to recover. Now the fundamental theorem statement with sigma/env. Let's write it:

```
Definition value_env (benv? no) ...
Theorem fundamental :
  forall Delta Gamma t T,
    has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
    forall d benv rho sigma1 sigma2 eta1 eta2,
      (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (sigma1 X)) ->
      (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (sigma2 X)) ->
      (forall X, value_rel (rho X)) ->
      (forall y, locally_closed_tm (eta1 y)) ->
      (forall y, locally_closed_tm (eta2 y)) ->

- i≠k: d+k-i ≠ d (since k≠i and nat arithmetic; if i>k, d+k-i < d so ≠ d; if i<k, >d) → benv (d+k-i) ✓.

Since the two sides differ only in rel_T1/rel_T2, and the existence is under the same step relation, we can prove equivalence by unfolding and using IH1/IH2 with the specific arguments. We can write a generic lemma: if for all a b, A a b <-> A' a b and B a b <-> B' a b, then (value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall a1 a2, A a1 a2 -> exists ..., B v1 v2) <-> (value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall a1 a2, A' a1 a2 -> exists ..., B' v1 v2). All case: LHS rel_ty (All (open_ty_rec (S k) FVarX T1)) depth benv rho' = value ∧ value ∧ forall U1 U2 R0 lc lc vrel, exists w1 w2, tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 ∧ ... ∧ rel_ty (open_ty_rec (S k) FVarX T1) (S depth) (insert depth R0 benv) rho' w1 w2.

Need IH with k:=S k, d:=d? and depth? The invariant: in the All case, the rel_ty depth for the body is S depth where depth = S d + k (the depth of the All type). So after increment, depth' = S (S d + k) = S d + S k. ✓ matches invariant for k'=S k, d'=d. And the inserted env: LHS body env = insert depth R0 benv = insert (S d + k) R0 benv. Careful: the IH applies to `rel_ty (open_ty_rec k' FVarX T1) (S d + k') benv_body rho'` vs `rel_ty T1 (S d + k') (insert d R benv_body) rho`, where in our case benv_body = insert depth R0 benv and k'=S k. Insertions at different positions d and S d + k (note S d + k > d since k≥0, actually S d + k ≥ d+1 > d). Inserting R at level d and R0 at level S d+k: if we insert R0 first at level S d+k then R at level d, vs insert R first at level d then R0 at level S d+k — the second insertion position shifts? Our `insert d R benv` is defined as `fun j => if j =? d then R else benv j`, which does NOT shift indices. Ah right! Since it's a function keyed by absolute levels, insertion doesn't shift. So `insert d R (insert (S d + k) R0 benv)` vs `insert (S d + k) R0 (insert d R benv)`: at j=d: first gives R (since insert d R ... d = R regardless), second: insert R0 at S d+k doesn't affect d (since S d+k ≠ d) → R. At j=S d+k: first: insert d R (insert ... R0 benv) at S d+k = (insert ... R0 benv) (S d+k) = R0; second: R0. Other j: both benv j. So they're equal pointwise. Again need extensionality/pointwise-equivalence to use the IH. Sigh. We can handle this with the rel_ty_ext lemma. Let's define:
```
Definition env_equiv (e1 e2 : nat -> Rel) : Prop :=
  forall j t1 t2, e1 j t1 t2 <-> e2 j t1 t2.
Definition rho_equiv (r1 r2 : atom -> Rel) : Prop :=
  forall X t1 t2, r1 X t1 t2 <-> r2 X t1 t2.
```
Prove by induction on T:
```
Lemma rel_ty_ext : forall T d e1 e2 r1 r2,
  env_equiv e1 e2 -> rho_equiv r1 r2 ->
  forall t1 t2, rel_ty T d e1 r1 t1 t2 <-> rel_ty T d e2 r2 t1 t2.
```
Proof:
- BVar: apply Henv.
- FVar: apply Hrho.
- Arrow T1 T2: unfold rel_ty. Need to be careful with the Fixpoint unfolding; `simpl` should unfold for concrete T=Arrow. Use `simpl`. Then:
  `(value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall a1 a2, rel_ty T1 d e1 r1 a1 a2 -> exists v1 v2, step* ... ∧ rel_ty T2 d e1 r1 v1 v2) <-> ... e2 r2 ...`.
  Apply IH1 and IH2. Write a helper lemma `arrow_ext` that takes pointwise iff for A/A' and B/B' and gives this.
  Actually the exists part: `forall a1 a2, A a1 a2 -> exists v1 v2, P v1 v2 ∧ B v1 v2`. If A<->A' and B<->B', equivalence is firstorder: from left, given a1 a2 and A' a1 a2, get A via IH1, get exists, then use IH2's direction to convert B. `firstorder` might handle it if we provide the pointwise hypotheses instantiated? We can do:
  ```
  split; intros H; destruct H as [Hv1 [Hv2 Hf]]; repeat split; auto;
    intros a1 a2 Ha; 
    assert (Ha' : A a1 a2) by (apply IH1; auto);  (* direction *)
    destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha') as (v1 & v2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & HB);
    exists v1, v2; repeat split; auto; apply IH2; auto.
  ```
  But applying IH1 requires it as a hypothesis with the right direction; we have IH1 : forall t1 t2, ... <-> ...; use `(proj1 (IH1 a1 a2))` etc.
- All T1: similar but now the envs are extended: `insert d R e1` vs `insert d R e2`, and rho unchanged. Need IH with these envs, requiring env_equiv (insert d R e1) (insert d R e2): intro j; destruct (Nat.eqb j d); [reflexivity | apply Henv]. The relation bodies are:
  `value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall U1 U2 R lc lc vr, exists w1 w2, P ∧ rel_ty T1 (S d) (insert d R e1) r1 w1 w2`
  vs e2. Apply IH1 with env_equiv (insert...). firstorder-style.

```
Fixpoint rel_ty_fuel (fuel : nat) (T : ty) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel :=
  match fuel with
  | 0 => fun _ _ => False
  | S f =>
    match T with
    | Ty_BVar i => fun _ _ => False
    | Ty_FVar X => rho X
    | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ... rel_ty_fuel f T1 ... rel_ty_fuel f T2 ...
    | Ty_All T1 =>
        ... forall X U1 U2 R, fresh X -> ... erel_fuel f (open_ty T1 (FVar X)) (rho[X:=R]) ...
    end
  end
with erel_fuel (fuel : nat) (T : ty) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel := ...
```

Fuel seems the most pragmatic. Let's flesh out the fuel approach.

```
Fixpoint ty_size (T : ty) : nat :=
  match T with
  | BVar _ | FVar _ => 1
  | Arrow T1 T2 => S (ty_size T1 + ty_size T2)
  | All T1 => S (ty_size T1)
  end.
```

Define:
```
Fixpoint rel_ty (fuel : nat) (T : ty) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel :=
  match fuel with
  | 0 => fun _ _ => False
  | S f =>
    match T with
    | Ty_BVar _ => fun _ _ => False
    | Ty_FVar X => rho X
    | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
        fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
          forall a1 a2, rel_ty f T1 rho a1 a2 ->
            exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ rel_ty f T2 rho v1 v2
    | Ty_All T1 =>
        fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
          forall (X : atom) (U1 U2 : ty) (R : Rel),
            (forall Y, rho Y ...)? (* fresh condition? *)
            ~ In X (free_ty_vars (open...))? 
            ...
    end
  end.
```

The term annotations are U1; the relation ignores them. This matches the parametricity argument! rel_ty (S f) (Ty_All T1) rho v1 v2 :=

    fresh X? ->
    locally_closed_ty U1 -> locally_closed_ty U2 ->

```
Inductive rel_ty : nat -> ty -> (atom -> Rel) -> tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  | rel_bvar : ...
  | rel_fvar : forall fuel n X rho t1 t2? ...
  | rel_arrow : forall fuel T1 T2 rho v1 v2,
      value v1 -> value v2 ->
      (forall a1 a2, rel_ty fuel T1 rho a1 a2 ->
         exists v1' v2', tm_app v1 a1 -->* v1' /\ tm_app v2 a2 -->* v2' /\ rel_ty fuel T2 rho v1' v2') ->
      rel_ty (S fuel) (Arrow T1 T2) rho v1 v2
  | rel_all : forall fuel T1 rho v1 v2,
      value v1 -> value v2 ->
      (forall X U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
         exists w1 w2, tm_tapp v1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tm_tapp v2 U2 -->* w2 /\
            rel_ty fuel (open_ty T1 (FVar X)) (rho[X:=R]) w1 w2) ->
      rel_ty (S fuel) (All T1) rho v1 v2.
```
The recursive occurrences are inside `exists` and `forall`, in *positive* positions? ```
Program Fixpoint rel_ty (T : ty) (rho : atom -> Rel) {measure (ty_size T)} : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => fun _ _ => False
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall a1 a2,
       rel_ty T1 rho a1 a2 -> exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ rel_ty T2 rho v1 v2
  | Ty_All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall X U1 U2 R,
       ~ In X (free_ty_vars T1) -> locally_closed_ty U1 -> ... ->
       exists w1 w2, tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ ... /\ rel_ty (open_ty T1 (FVar X)) (rho[X:=R]) w1 w2
  end.
Next Obligation. (* size T1 < size (Arrow T1 T2) *) ...
Next Obligation. (* size (open_ty T1 FVarX) < size (All T1) *) ...
```
But the occurrence of rel_ty in the arrow case under the `forall a1 a2, rel_ty T1 ... -> ...` is fine for a Fixpoint (it's a function definition, not an Inductive; recursion in function bodies can occur anywhere as long as structurally decreasing). Fixpoint rel_ty_aux (n : nat) : forall T, ty_size T <= n -> Rel :=

```

```
Fixpoint rel_ty_aux (n : nat) (T : ty) (H : ty_size T <= n) (rho : atom -> Rel) {struct n} : Rel :=
  match n as n return ty_size T <= n -> Rel with
  | 0 => fun H => fun _ _ => False  (* impossible if size T >=1, but T could... size always ≥1 so H: 1 ≤ 0 impossible *)
  | S n' =>
      fun H =>
      match T as T return ty_size T <= S n' -> Rel with
      | BVar _ => fun _ _ _ => False
      | FVar X => fun _ => rho X
      | Arrow T1 T2 => fun H t1 t2 =>
          value t1 /\ value t2 /\
          forall a1 a2, rel_ty_aux n' T1 (lemmas) rho a1 a2 ->
            exists v1 v2, ... rel_ty_aux n' T2 (lemmas) rho v1 v2
      | All T1 => fun H t1 t2 =>
          value t1 /\ value t2 /\
          forall X U1 U2 R, ... -> exists w1 w2, ... rel_ty_aux n' (open_ty T1 FVarX) (lemma) (rho[X:=R]) w1 w2
      end H
  end H.
```
The recursive calls use n' (structurally smaller than n). The dependently typed match with H is a bit intricate but doable. Then define `rel_ty T rho := rel_ty_aux (ty_size T) T (le_n) rho`. The unfolding lemmas need to compute rel_ty_aux (size T) with T = Arrow T1 T2: size T = S (s1+s2), so n = S n' with n' = s1+s2. The recursive calls inside use rel_ty_aux n' T1 with proof `size T1 ≤ n'`, while rel_ty T1 uses rel_ty_aux (size T1) T1. These have different n (n' vs size T1) → need fuel irrelevance again! Damn.

Unless we make the recursive calls use fuel exactly `size T1`... guard checker on n rejects.

Actually, there's a subtlety: with Equations `by wf`, the generated function is well-defined, and equation lemmas hold. But `simpl` won't reduce; we use `rewrite rel_ty_equation_...` or `simp rel_ty` (Equations provides `simp` tactic). We should be able to use the equation lemmas manually.

Plan: define:
```
Equations rel_val (T : ty) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel by wf (ty_size T) lt :=
  rel_val (Ty_BVar i) rho := fun _ _ => False;
  rel_val (Ty_FVar X) rho := rho X;
  rel_val (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) rho :=
     fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall a1 a2, rel_val T1 rho a1 a2 ->
          exists v1 v2, step* ... /\ rel_val T2 rho v1 v2;
  rel_val (Ty_All T1) rho :=
     fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
        forall X U1 U2 R, ~ In X (free_ty_vars_ty T1) -> lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
          exists w1 w2, tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tm_tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\
             rel_val (open_ty T1 (Ty_FVar X)) (fun Y => if Y =? X then R else rho Y) w1 w2.
```
The decreasing obligation for open_ty T1 FVarX: need ty_size (open_ty T1 FVarX) < ty_size (All T1) = S (size T1). Need size(open...) = size T1 (or ≤). Prove lemma first.

Then `erel T rho := fun t1 t2 => exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T rho v1 v2`.

Alpha-renaming might be unavoidable in the named-free-variable approach. Let's weigh:
- benv approach: no atom renaming needed. Need:
  (a) rel_val with benv (depth/list or function).
  (b) semantic lemma: rel_val (open_ty_rec k FVarX T) at depth D+k with benv ≡ rel_val T at depth D+k with benv updated at level D (or list insertion).
  (c) rel_val env extensionality to handle update commutativity.
  (d) Type substitution into terms commits (for T_TAbs): we need the IH conclusion at type open_ty T1 FVarX with sigma' X:=U, giving erel at that type between inst terms = open_tm_ty body1 U1 etc. Then the semantic lemma converts the relation at open_ty T1 FVarX to T1 with benv push, matching the All relation's body requirement. Yes.
  (e) All relation quantifies over U1,U2,R but no X.
- free-var approach: no benv lemma but needs renaming.

The benv approach seems more self-contained and avoids L. Let's also check the All relation in the benv approach can be defined on values directly requiring `value v1 ∧ value v2` and forall U1,U2,R: erel at T1 with pushed benv of `tapp v1 U1` and `tapp v2 U2`. In T_TAbs, erel requires tapp (tabs body1) U1 -->* w1; use ST_TAppTabs. The IH gives erel at open_ty T1 FVarX between open_tm_ty body1 U1 and body2 U2. The semantic lemma converts the relation *type*; but the terms in IH are exactly open_tm_ty body1 U1 and body2 U2? Yes: inst sigma' eta (open_tm_ty body FVarX) = open_tm_ty body1 U1 as computed. And the relation at open_ty T1 FVarX is what IH gives. We need to convert erel at open_ty T1 FVarX (depth d) into erel at T1 with pushed benv (same terms). The semantic lemma gives rel_val equivalence at all pairs; erel is exists over rel_val, so equivalence of erel follows. Good.

Then the All body requirement of erel at T1 (pushed benv) between `tapp (tabs body1) U1` and `tapp (tabs body2) U2` follows from the IH erel between the opened bodies by multistep prefix. ✓.

So benv it is. Now, benv representation and the semantic lemma. Let's design to minimize extensionality pain.

Use absolute levels: `benv : nat -> Rel`, with rel_val T d benv rho:
- BVar i => benv (d - 1 - i)
- FVar X => rho X
- Arrow => ...
- All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R -> erel T1 (S d) (fun j => if j =? d then R else benv j) rho (tapp t1 U1) (tapp t2 U2)

Top-level d=0; BVar never occurs free in wf types. Since rel_val is defined by wf recursion on ty_size, now the All case calls rel_val on T1 (subterm, fine) — no opening needed in the relation! Great: the relation is structurally recursive on T, no fuel issues, no open_ty. So Equations can define it with a simple structural recursion? rel_val T d benv rho returns Rel; T is structurally decreasing in Arrow (T1,T2) and All (T1). Yes! A plain Fixpoint works, no wf measure needed. Because the All case no longer opens the body. Excellent. So we can avoid Equations entirely and use a plain Fixpoint:

```
Fixpoint rel_val (T : ty) (d : nat) (benv : nat -> Rel) (rho : atom -> Rel) {struct T} : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => benv (d - 1 - i)
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
       forall a1 a2, rel_val T1 d benv rho a1 a2 ->
         exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T2 d benv rho v1 v2
  | Ty_All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
       forall U1 U2 R, locally_closed_ty U1 -> locally_closed_ty U2 -> value_rel R ->
         exists w1 w2, tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tm_tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\
           rel_val T1 (S d) (fun j => if j =? d then R else benv j) rho w1 w2
  end.
```
Guard checker: recursive calls on T1/T2 subterms ✓.

Then `erel T d benv rho t1 t2 := exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T d benv rho v1 v2`.

Wait: do we need `value` conjuncts in rel_val Arrow/All? erel's v1,v2 are supposed to be values; rel_val Arrow/All includes value. At TVar, rel_val = rho X; we assume rho X is a value relation. At BVar, benv entries assumed value relations. So we maintain value_rel for rho and benv. Good.

Now the semantic lemma. We need:
`rel_val (open_ty_rec k (FVar X) T) d benv' rho' ≡ rel_val T d benv rho` for appropriate d, benv', rho'. With the depth convention, and open_ty_rec starting at k. Let's re-derive the invariant with this rel_val (note: rel_val's d is depth *outside* T; the All case increments). At the top of the lemma, suppose T is interpreted at depth d0 and opened with k0. Under a internal Alls, T's subjective depth = d0 + a, and open_ty_rec's k = k0 + a. BVar indices in T at that position: free indices refer to the binders of open_ty's target. open_ty_rec replaces index k = k0+a at depth d0+a; the replaced binder is at level? rel_val at depth d0+a maps BVar i to level (d0+a)-1-i. The replaced index k=k0+a → level (d0+a)-1-(k0+a) = d0-1-k0. Constant L. But if d0=0 and k0=0, L = 0-1-0 = 0 (truncated) — that's wrong because BVar0 at depth 0 maps to level 0 by truncation, but in the unopened type at depth 0, BVar0 would refer to a binder not present? At depth 0, rel_val BVar i = benv (0-1-i) = benv 0 (truncated). Hmm, the depth convention should be: when interpreting a type T that is the body of an All at depth d, we call rel_val T (S d); so T's BVar0 maps to level (S d)-1-0 = d, the new binder. In the semantic lemma, T (the body) is interpreted at depth S d (not d0=0). Let's set d0 = S d. Then L = d0 - 1 - k0 = S d - 1 - 0 = d. Good.

So lemma statement:
For all T, k, d, benv, rho, X, R:
`rel_val (open_ty_rec k (Ty_FVar X) T) (S d + k) benv rho' ≡ rel_val T (S d + k) (fun j => if j =? d then R else benv j) rho`
where rho' = fun Y => if Y =? X then R else rho Y, and X should be fresh? Not needed for this lemma because we're just relating the type expressions; the opened type contains FVar X which is interpreted by rho' X = R. If T also contains FVar X free, then in the opened type those occurrences are still FVar X and get R; in the RHS T under rho they'd get rho X. So the lemma REQUIRES that X is not free in T (or that rho X = R). Add hypothesis `~ In X (free_ty_vars_ty T)`. Then rho and rho' agree on T's free vars. But the lemma proof by induction on T will need rho_ext for the subterms? Let's see: BVar: no rho. FVar Y: LHS rho' Y; RHS rho Y; if Y ≠ X (which follows from X not free in T and Y free in T? Actually if T = FVar Y and X not free in T, then Y≠X; need rho' Y = rho Y). Use Nat.eqb. Arrow: IH on T1 with X not free in T1 (follows from X not free in Arrow), same depth etc. All: IH on T1 with X not free in T1; the env updates commute (insert R0 at level S d+k vs insert R at level d). Compare:
LHS All body env: insert (S d + k?) Wait, in the All case at depth D = S d + k, rel_val (All T1) D benv rho' = forall R0, erel T1 (S D) (insert D R0 benv) rho'. The opened All: open_ty_rec k FVarX (All T1) = All (open_ty_rec (S k) FVarX T1); rel_val at depth D with benv. All body env: insert D R0 benv, depth S D. The IH (with k'=S k, d'=d, D'=S d + S k = S D) gives:
rel_val (open_ty_rec (S k) FVarX T1) (S D) benv_body rho' ≡ rel_val T1 (S D) (insert d R benv_body) rho
where benv_body = insert D R0 benv.
RHS of the All case: rel_val (All T1) D (insert d R benv) rho = forall R0, rel_val T1 (S D) (insert D R0 (insert d R benv)) rho.
So we need env equivalence between `insert d R (insert D R0 benv)` and `insert D R0 (insert d R benv)`. Since D = S d + k ≥ d+1 > d, these are equal at every level: 
- j=d: LHS R; RHS insert D R0 ... at d = (if d =? D then R0 else insert d R benv d) = R (since d≠D).
- j=D: LHS insert d R (insert D R0 benv) D = (insert D R0 benv) D = R0 (since d≠D); RHS R0.
- else: both benv j.
Pointwise equal, needing eqb arithmetic.

So the semantic lemma needs env equivalence `env_eq` and the induction hypothesis at T1 gives relation equivalence at subterms with the *specific* envs, but the envs differ. We can incorporate env_eq into the induction statement: instead of proving the lemma for exactly these envs, prove:

`rel_val T D benv1 rho1 ≡ rel_val T D benv2 rho2` whenever `env_eq benv1 benv2` and `rho_eq_on (free_ty_vars_ty T) rho1 rho2`.

Then the semantic lemma follows: show env_eq between the two All-body envs using arithmetic, and rho agreement on free vars.

So let's prove two extensionality lemmas:
1. `rel_val_env_ext`: forall T d benv1 benv2 rho1 rho2, (forall j t1 t2, benv1 j t1 t2 <-> benv2 j t1 t2) -> (forall X, In X (free_ty_vars_ty T) -> forall t1 t2, rho1 X t1 t2 <-> rho2 X t1 t2) -> forall t1 t2, rel_val T d benv1 rho1 t1 t2 <-> rel_val T d benv2 rho2 t1 t2.
   Prove by structural induction on T. BVar: use env H. FVar X: free X in T, use rho H. Arrow: IH1/IH2, plus arrow relation ext. All: push R; need env H' for `insert d R benv1` vs `insert d R benv2`: pointwise from H (if j=d, both R; else H j). Need rho H for T1's free vars: free_ty_vars_ty T1 ⊆ free_ty_vars_ty (All T1) (obvious; T1's free vars are the same as All T1's free vars). Use IH1. Then firstorder-ish.
2. `rel_val_rho_agree`: could be a special case of 1 with benv same. Fine.

The semantic lemma with X fresh and env comparison:
`rel_val (open_ty_rec k FVarX T) (S d + k) benv (rho[X:=R]) ≡ rel_val T (S d + k) (insert d R benv) rho`, under X ∉ free_ty_vars T.
Proof by induction on T:
- BVar i: as computed; need arithmetic and eqb simplification. Note LHS's rel_val is at type `if i =? k then FVar X else BVar i` — this is not syntactically a match that the Fixpoint will reduce unless we destruct (Nat.eqb i k). We can case on `Nat.eqb i k` using `destruct (Nat.eqb i k) eqn:E`; then compute open_ty_rec. Actually open_ty_rec simplifies with the if; `simpl` may reduce `if Nat.eqb k i then ...`. We'll manage.
- FVar Y: need Y ≠ X from X not free in T (T=FVar Y → free vars = [Y], and X∉[Y]); then rho' Y = rho Y.
- Arrow: apply IH1, IH2, then arrow ext.
- All: as above: use IH with k+1 on T1, X fresh (from X∉free(All T1)=free T1); the envs: LHS body env = insert D R0 benv where D = S d + k; RHS body env = insert D R0 (insert d R benv); IH's RHS env = insert d R (insert D R0 benv). Need env_eq between them (arithmetic). Then apply IH. The relation equivalence for all pairs gives the All relation equivalence.
So we need helper lemmas for relation equivalence under pointwise iff for Arrow and All shapes. Because rel_val Arrow is a function returning a Prop with foralls/exists. We can prove:
```
Lemma arrow_rel_equiv : forall (A A' B B' : Rel),
  (forall t1 t2, A t1 t2 <-> A' t1 t2) ->
  (forall t1 t2, B t1 t2 <-> B' t1 t2) ->
  forall t1 t2,
    (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall a1 a2, A a1 a2 ->
       exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ B v1 v2)
    <->
    (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall a1 a2, A' a1 a2 ->
       exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ B' v1 v2).
```
Proof: firstorder with the iff hypotheses instantiated; we may need to help. `firstorder` can use hypotheses of the form forall t1 t2, A t1 t2 <-> A' t1 t2. It should handle. But note the exists witnesses and conjunctions—firstorder should work. If not, manual.

Similarly `all_rel_equiv`:
```
forall A A', (forall t1 t2, A t1 t2 <-> A' t1 t2) ->
 forall t1 t2,
  (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
     exists w1 w2, tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\ A w1 w2)
  <->
  (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall U1 U2 R, ... /\ A' w1 w2).
```
firstorder.

These helper lemmas make the extensionality proofs clean: in each case, `apply arrow_rel_equiv; [apply IH1 ... | apply IH2 ...]` or similar.

Now, the fundamental theorem. Let's write it carefully with all hypotheses.

Definitions:
- `Rel := tm -> tm -> Prop`.
- `value_rel R := forall t1 t2, R t1 t2 -> value t1 /\ value t2`.
- `rel_val T d benv rho`.
- `erel T d benv rho t1 t2 := exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T d benv rho v1 v2`.

Type substitution: define `ty_subst_env (sigma : atom -> ty) : ty -> ty` (structural), and `tm_ty_subst_env`, `tm_subst_env`.
- inst sigma eta t := tm_subst_env eta (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).

Free vars:
- `free_ty_vars_ty T : list atom`
- `free_ty_vars_tm t : list atom`
- `free_tm_vars t : list atom`

Freshness:
- `context_dom Gamma := map fst Gamma`
- `context_free_ty_vars Gamma := flat_map (fun p => free_ty_vars_ty (snd p)) Gamma`

Fundamental theorem statement:
```
Theorem fundamental :
  forall Delta Gamma t T,
    has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
    forall d benv rho sigma1 sigma2 eta1 eta2,
      (forall j, value_rel (benv j)) ->
      (forall X, value_rel (rho X)) ->
      (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (sigma1 X)) ->
      (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (sigma2 X)) ->
      (forall y, locally_closed_tm (eta1 y)) ->
      (forall y, locally_closed_tm (eta2 y)) ->
      (forall y S, In (y,S) Gamma -> rel_val S d benv rho (eta1 y) (eta2 y)) ->
      erel T d benv rho (inst sigma1 eta1 t) (inst sigma2 eta2 t).
```

Now, the lc companion: we need lc of inst terms for value and steps. Let's formulate:
```
Lemma has_type_lc :
  forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (sigma X)) ->
  locally_closed_tm (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).
```
This is independent of d/rho/eta. And then `lc_tm_subst_env`: if all eta images lc and lc t then lc (tm_subst_env eta t).

Then in the fundamental theorem, we get lc of `tm_ty_subst_env sigma t` from has_type_lc, and lc of `inst sigma eta t` from tm_subst_env lc with eta images lc. Good.

Now, the lc companion proof needs `wf_ty_subst_lc` and the reverse opening lemmas. Let's detail:
- `lc_ty_mono`: lc_ty_at k T -> k ≤ k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.
- `lc_ty_open_fvar_rev`: lc_ty_at k (open_ty_rec k (Ty_FVar X) T) -> lc_ty_at (S k) T.
- `lc_tm_mono`: lc_tm_at K k t -> K ≤ K' -> k ≤ k' -> lc_tm_at K' k' t. (Both parameters monotone? Type binders and term binders.)
- `lc_tm_open_ty_fvar_rev`: lc_tm_at K k (open_tm_ty_rec K? ...). We derived with matching j: `lc_tm_at j k (open_tm_ty_rec j (Ty_FVar X) t) -> lc_tm_at (S j) k t`. Here j is the type binder depth parameter and also the opening index. Note lc_tm_at's K at the root should equal the open index j. In our use, the opened term is `tm_ty_subst_env sigma' (open_tm_ty body FVarX)` where open_tm_ty = open_tm_ty_rec 0, and sigma' substitutes types. Now, one crucial check: Does the fundamental theorem actually hold for terms with arbitrary `Gamma` and term envs where env relation uses rel_val (a value relation)? Let's carefully go through the cases to ensure the relation is strong enough for T_Var/T_Abs/T_App/T_TAbs/T_TApp/T_Choice.

Definitions recap:
```
rel_val T d benv rho :=
 match T with
 | BVar i => benv (d-1-i)
 | FVar X => rho X
 | Arrow T1 T2 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
     forall a1 a2, rel_val T1 d benv rho a1 a2 ->
       exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T2 d benv rho v1 v2
 | All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
     forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
       exists w1 w2, tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tm_tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\
         rel_val T1 (S d) (insert d R benv) rho w1 w2
 end.

erel T d benv rho t1 t2 := exists v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T d benv rho v1 v2.
```

So the list formulation is much cleaner! Let's switch to lists:
```
rel_val T benv rho :=
  match T with
  | BVar i => nth i benv (fun _ _ => False)
  | FVar X => rho X
  | Arrow T1 T2 => ...
  | All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧
      forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
        exists w1 w2, tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 ∧ tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 ∧
          rel_val T1 (R :: benv) rho w1 w2
  end
```

Fixpoint bvars_below (k : nat) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | BVar i => i < k
  | FVar _ => True
  | Arrow T1 T2 => bvars_below k T1 /\ bvars_below k T2
  | All T1 => bvars_below (S k) T1
  end.
```

Definition replace_at (k : nat) (R : Rel) (benv : list Rel) : list Rel :=

```
Fixpoint replace_at (k:nat) (R:Rel) (benv:list Rel) : list Rel :=
  match k, benv with
  | 0, [] => []
  | 0, b::bs => R :: bs
  | S k', [] => []
  | S k', b::bs => b :: replace_at k' R bs
  end.
```
Then `replace_at (S k') R (b::bs) = b :: replace_at k' R bs` definitionally, with b=R? In our comparison, `replace_at (S k) RU (R :: benv)` = R :: replace_at k RU benv definitionally! Yes! Let's verify the All case of the substitution lemma carefully with benv possibly non-nil and U lc:
LHS: rel_val (open_ty_rec k U (All T1)) benv rho = rel_val (All (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1)) benv rho = fun t1 t2 => value... ∧ forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R -> exists w1 w2, tapp... ∧ rel_val (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1) (R :: benv) rho w1 w2.
RHS: rel_val (All T1) (replace_at k RU benv) rho = fun t1 t2 => value... ∧ forall U1 U2 R, ... exists w1 w2, ... ∧ rel_val T1 (R :: replace_at k RU benv) rho w1 w2.
IH (k' = S k, benv' = R :: benv): rel_val (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1) (R::benv) rho ≡ rel_val T1 (replace_at (S k) (rel_val U (R::benv) rho) (R::benv)) rho.
By lc_env_irrel for U (lc at 0), rel_val U (R::benv) rho ≡ rel_val U benv rho = RU. Need rel_val T1 env with replace_at (S k) RU (R::benv). And replace_at (S k) RU (R::benv) = R :: replace_at k RU benv by the recursive definition (since the head is R and the function ignores it except consing). ✓ definitionally. So RHS env matches. The outer All structure (value, forall...) is syntactically identical once the body relations are equivalent. To prove this in Coq, we again need an all_rel_equiv lemma: if two body relations A,A' are pointwise equivalent, the All relations are equivalent. Standard.
So the substitution lemma holds with U lc.

Now, the relation definition with lists:
```
Definition Rel := tm -> tm -> Prop.
Definition rel_env := list Rel.

Fixpoint rel_val (T : ty) (benv : rel_env) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => nth i benv (fun _ _ => False)
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ...
  | Ty_All T1 => fun t1 t2 => value t1 /\ value t2 /\
      forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
        exists w1 w2, tapp t1 U1 -->* w1 /\ tapp t2 U2 -->* w2 /\
          rel_val T1 (R :: benv) rho w1 w2
  end.
```

Then we expand the All and Arrow relations as described. We also need to know `wf_ty [] U -> lc_ty U` for applying the All relation at U: the All relation's erel uses `tm_tapp v1 U -->* w1` requiring ST_TAppTabs's `locally_closed_ty U`; wf_ty [] U -> lc_ty_at 0 U. We need the wf_ty_lc lemma for this. Also the relation's All case quantifies lc U1 U2; fine.

Also the target needs lc of U for multi_tapp congruence. Same lemma.

Now, the `erel` for the target: fundamental theorem gives erel T nil rho t t with T = All (Arrow BVar0 BVar0). Expand erel: exists v1 v2, t -->* v1, t -->* v2, rel_val All nil rho v1 v2. Then expand rel_val All: value v1... forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R -> exists w1 w2, tapp v1 U1 -->* w1, tapp v2 U2 -->* w2, rel_val (Arrow BVar0 BVar0) (R :: nil) rho w1 w2. Choose U1=U2=U, R := rel_val U nil rho? Or R := the singleton relation `fun a b => a = v ∧ b = v`. Which one should we choose? We need rel_val (Arrow BVar0 BVar0) (R::nil) rho w1 w2 to eventually give that app w1 v -->* v. The BVar0 relation is R. So R should be the relation we want at the argument/result: R a b := (a = v ∧ b = v)? Then the arrow application with a1=a2=v gives R of results, i.e., result = v. And R must be value_rel: a=v,b=v -> value a,value b ✓. Also tapp v1 U -->* w1 etc. Then app (tapp v1 U) v -->* ... Let's compute: rel_val Arrow BVar0 BVar0 at env R::nil:
fun t1 t2 => value t1 ∧ value t2 ∧ forall a1 a2, R a1 a2 -> exists z1 z2, app t1 a1 -->* z1 ∧ app t2 a2 -->* z2 ∧ R z1 z2.
Given w1,w2 related, apply to v,v (R v v by reflexivity: v = v). Get z1,z2 with app w1 v -->* z1 ∧ R z1 z2 → z1 = v. Then compose:
t -->* v1, tapp congruence: tapp t U -->* tapp v1 U (needs lc U at each step, and v1 value? multi_tapp congruence requires t -->* v1 and lc U; each ST_TApp needs lc U and the premise t --> t'; fine).
tapp v1 U -->* w1 (from All erel).
Then app congruence left: app (tapp t U) v -->* app w1 v (multi_app_l with lc v; each ST_App1 requires locally_closed_tm v — value v gives lc).
Then app w1 v -->* z1 = v. Compose all multi steps. ✓.

Alternatively choose R := rel_val U nil rho, then BVar0 ~ U's semantics; but then applying to v requires rel_val U nil rho v v, which may not hold (e.g., if U is an arrow type, rel_val arrow v v requires functional behavior, not true for an arbitrary value). So the singleton relation is the right choice. Note R is independent of U; that's the free theorem magic. ✓.

Important: In the All relation expansion, the relation R is quantified with value_rel R; our singleton R is a value relation. Good.

Now let's make sure the fundamental theorem itself is actually true with the list/benv relation. Let's run through T_TAbs once more with the exact relation and the substitution lemma:

But in the commutation lemma at deeper positions the opening index may be >0; the plugged eta y occurs at a position with type depth K (since eta y is inserted by term substitution, and the surrounding term has type binder depth K); lc_tm_at 0 0 (eta y) gives lc at the *local* K? We can weaken lc_tm_at 0 0 to lc_tm_at K k' for any K,k' (monotonicity). Then open_tm_ty_rec K U (eta y) = eta y by the lemma. ✓. So the commutation lemma `tm_subst_env eta (open_tm_ty_rec k U t) = open_tm_ty_rec k U (tm_subst_env eta t)` needs, at the tm_fvar y leaf, open_tm_ty_rec k U (eta y) = eta y with k = the current opening index. But what is the relationship between the current opening index k and the lc type depth K of eta y? The lc of eta y is at 0 0, and we can weaken to K=k? lc_tm_at k k (eta y) by monotonicity from 0 0. Then the lemma `lc_tm_at K k' t -> open_tm_ty_rec K U t = t` with K=k gives open identity. ✓. Great.

Similarly for tm_ty_subst commutation with open_tm_rec at the fvar leaf: RHS open_tm_rec k (tm_ty_subst_env sigma u) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma (fvar y)) = open_tm_rec k ... (fvar y) = fvar y; LHS = fvar y. No issue.

For tm_subst_env with open_tm at fvar leaf: open_tm_rec k (eta x) (eta y) = eta y requires lc_tm_at 0 0 (eta y) → open_tm_rec k (eta x) (eta y) = eta y. The earlier lemma `lc_tm_at K k t -> open_tm_rec k u t = t` with K arbitrary, k=the opening index, k'=0? We proved `lc_tm_at K k t -> open_tm_rec k u t = t`? Let's recheck: we need the opening index equals lc's term depth param. If eta y is lc at 0 0, mono gives lc_tm_at K k (eta y) for any K,k. Then open_tm_rec k u (eta y) = eta y. ✓. But careful: our lemma statement was `lc_tm_at K k t -> open_tm_rec k u t = t` where the opening index equals the lc term-depth k. Mono lets us choose k equal to the opening index. ✓.

OK. Also for open_tm_ty with U = Ty_FVar X in the T_TAbs equality: the commutation `tm_ty_subst_env sigma (open_tm_ty_rec k (Ty_FVar X) t) = open_tm_ty_rec k (ty_subst_env sigma (FVar X)) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t)` holds; then ty_subst_env sigma (FVar X) = sigma X = U when sigma' X = U. ✓.

Let's detail has_type_lc:
```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma X)) ->
  lc_tm (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).
```
Proof by induction on has_type.
- T_Var: term fvar x; lc_tm (fvar x) = lc_tm_at 0 0 (fvar x) via lc_tm_fvar. ✓.
- T_Abs: term abs T1 t2. Need lc_tm_at 0 0 (abs (sigma T1) (sigma t2)): lc_ty_at 0 (sigma T1) via wf_ty_subst_lc (wf_ty Delta T1) and lc_tm_at 0 1 (sigma t2). IH on premise: has_type Delta (x::T1::Gamma) (open_tm t2 FVarx) T2 gives lc_tm_at 0 0 (sigma (open_tm t2 FVarx)) (sigma doesn't touch term fvar x; type subst commutes with open_tm: = tm_ty_subst_env sigma (open_tm_rec 0 (fvar x) t2) = open_tm_rec 0 (tm_ty_subst_env sigma (fvar x)=fvar x) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2) = open_tm (sigma t2) (fvar x)). Reverse term-opening lemma: lc_tm_at 0 0 (open_tm_rec 0 (fvar x) (sigma t2)) -> lc_tm_at 0 1 (sigma t2). ✓.
  Note: the IH's sigma hypothesis: same Delta, so lc on Delta ✓. 
- T_App: lc_tm_at 0 0 (app (sigma t1) (sigma t2)) from IH1, IH2. ✓.
- T_TAbs: term tabs t. Need lc_tm_at 0 0 (tabs (sigma t)) = lc_tm_at 1 0 (sigma t). IH on premise with Delta' = X::Delta and sigma' = sigma[X:=FVar X] where X fresh from L ∪ Delta. Hyp: for all Y in X::Delta, lc (sigma' Y). ✓. IH gives lc_tm_at 0 0 (tm_ty_subst_env sigma' (open_tm_ty t FVarX)). Commutation: = open_tm_ty_rec 0 (ty_subst_env sigma' (FVar X) = FVar X) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma' t) = open_tm_ty_rec 0 FVarX (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t) (using X∉free_ty_vars_tm t). Then reverse lemma `lc_tm_at 0 0 (open_tm_ty_rec 0 FVarX body') -> lc_tm_at 1 0 body'`. ✓.
  Need X fresh from free_ty_vars_tm t and Delta (for sigma' agreement on free vars t) and L (premise holds). We can choose X outside the union.
- T_TApp: term tapp t U. Need lc_tm_at 0 0 (tapp (sigma t) (sigma U)): lc of sigma t from IH on premise, lc_ty_at 0 (sigma U) from wf_ty Delta U via wf_ty_subst_lc. ✓.
- T_Choice: IH1, IH2. ✓.
Great.

Then the fundamental theorem's lc uses: `lc_tm (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t)` plus `lc_tm_subst_env eta` with eta images lc to get `lc_tm (inst sigma eta t)`. Since lc_tm = lc_tm_at 0 0. We can use a fixed hypothesis forall y, lc_tm (eta y) and mono. Simpler: prove by induction with statement `forall K k t, lc_tm_at K k t -> forall eta, (forall y, lc_tm (eta y)) -> lc_tm_at K k (tm_subst_env eta t)`. At fvar y: need lc_tm_at K k (eta y) from lc_tm (eta y) via mono (0≤K, 0≤k). ✓. Abs: IH with k:=S k. Tabs: K:=S K. ✓.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat := ...

```
Prove by induction: fold_right max 0 l ≥ each element. Use `Nat.le_max_r`, `fold_right` lemmas or prove `forall l x, In x l -> x <= fold_right max 0 l`. Fixpoint nth (n:nat) (l:list A) (default:A) {struct l} : A :=
  match n, l with
  | O, x :: l' => x
  | O, [] => default
  | S m, [] => default
  | S m, x :: l' => nth m l' default
  end.
```

Fixpoint replace_at (k:nat) (R:Rel) (l:list Rel) : list Rel :=
  match k, l with
  | 0, [] => []
  | 0, _::xs => R :: xs
  | S k', [] => []
  | S k', x::xs => x :: replace_at k' R xs
  end.
```
Then replace_at (S k') R (x::xs) = x :: replace_at k' R xs definitionally (the match reduces on S k' and cons). ✓.

Definitions:

```
(** * Free variables *)
Fixpoint free_ty_vars_ty (T : ty) : list atom :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar _ => []
  | Ty_FVar X => [X]
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => free_ty_vars_ty T1 ++ free_ty_vars_ty T2
  | Ty_All T1 => free_ty_vars_ty T1
  end.

Fixpoint free_ty_vars_tm (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar _ => []
  | tm_abs T t1 => free_ty_vars_ty T ++ free_ty_vars_tm t1
  | tm_app t1 t2 => free_ty_vars_tm t1 ++ free_ty_vars_tm t2
  | tm_tabs t1 => free_ty_vars_tm t1
  | tm_tapp t1 T => free_ty_vars_tm t1 ++ free_ty_vars_ty T
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => free_ty_vars_tm t1 ++ free_ty_vars_tm t2
  end.

Fixpoint free_tm_vars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_abs T t1 => free_tm_vars t1
  | tm_app t1 t2 => free_tm_vars t1 ++ free_tm_vars t2
  | tm_tabs t1 => free_tm_vars t1
  | tm_tapp t1 _ => free_tm_vars t1
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => free_tm_vars t1 ++ free_tm_vars t2
  end.
```

Note: free_tm_vars of tm_abs body includes term FVars; the binder is BVar, not FVar, so fine.

Type substitution env:
```
Fixpoint ty_subst_env (sigma : atom -> ty) (T : ty) : ty :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => Ty_BVar i
  | Ty_FVar X => sigma X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => Ty_Arrow (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (ty_subst_env sigma T2)
  | Ty_All T1 => Ty_All (ty_subst_env sigma T1)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env (sigma : atom -> ty) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (ty_subst_env sigma T)
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_subst_env (eta : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => eta x
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (tm_subst_env eta t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (tm_subst_env eta t1) (tm_subst_env eta t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => tm_tabs (tm_subst_env eta t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_subst_env eta t1) T
  | tm_choice t1 t2 => tm_choice (tm_subst_env eta t1) (tm_subst_env eta t2)
  end.

Definition inst (sigma : atom -> ty) (eta : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  tm_subst_env eta (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).
```

- identity substitutions.
- ty_subst_env agrees on free vars.
- commutations with openings.

Lemma free_ty_vars_open_ty_incl : forall T U X k,
  In X (free_ty_vars_ty (open_ty_rec k U T)) ->
  In X (free_ty_vars_ty T) \/ In X (free_ty_vars_ty U).

  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + right. apply H.
    + left. apply H.
  - left; simpl; auto.
  - apply in_app_iff in H. destruct H.
    + apply in_app_iff; left; apply IH1; auto.
    + apply in_app_iff; right; apply IH2; auto.
  - apply IH with (k := S k); auto.
Qed.
```

    + left; exact H.

```
Lemma wf_ty_subst_lc : forall Delta T,
  wf_ty Delta T ->
  forall sigma, (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma X)) ->
  lc_ty (ty_subst_env sigma T).
Proof.
  intros Delta T H.
  induction H as [Delta0 X0 Hin | Delta0 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | L Delta0 T0 Hpre IHpre];
    intros sigma Hsig; simpl.
  - apply Hsig; exact Hin.
  - constructor; auto.
  - set (X := fresh_atom (L ++ (Delta0 ++ free_ty_vars_ty T0))).
    assert (HfX : ~ In X (L ++ (Delta0 ++ free_ty_vars_ty T0))) by apply fresh_atom_notin.
    assert (HXL : ~ In X L) by apply (notin_app_l _ _ _ HfX).
    assert (HXD : ~ In X Delta0) by apply (notin_app_l _ _ _ (notin_app_r _ _ _ HfX)).
    assert (HXT : ~ In X (free_ty_vars_ty T0)) by apply (notin_app_r _ _ _ (notin_app_r _ _ _ HfX)).
    set (sigma' := fun Y => if Y =? X then Ty_FVar X else sigma Y).
    assert (Hsig' : forall Y, In Y (X :: Delta0) -> lc_ty (sigma' Y)).
    { intros Y HY. simpl in HY. destruct HY as [-> | HY].
      - unfold sigma'. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. constructor.
      - unfold sigma'. destruct (Nat.eqb Y X) eqn:E. constructor. apply Hsig; exact HY. }
    specialize (IHpre X HXL sigma' Hsig').
    assert (Hlc' : forall Y, In Y (free_ty_vars_ty T0) -> lc_ty (sigma' Y)).
    { intros Y HY.
      assert (YinD : In Y Delta0).
      { set (Z := fresh_atom (L ++ ([Y] ++ Delta0))).
        assert (HfZ : ~ In Z (L ++ ([Y] ++ Delta0))) by apply fresh_atom_notin.
        assert (HZL : ~ In Z L) by apply (notin_app_l _ _ _ HfZ).
        assert (HZY : Z <> Y).
        { intro Heq. subst Z.
          apply (notin_app_l _ _ _ (notin_app_r _ _ _ HfZ)). simpl. auto. }
        pose proof (wf_ty_free_vars _ _ (Hpre Z HZL) Y
                      (free_ty_vars_ty_open_mono T0 (Ty_FVar Z) 0 Y HY)) as Hin2.
        destruct Hin2 as [Heq | Hin2].
        + apply HZY. symmetry. exact Heq.
        + exact Hin2. }
      unfold sigma'. destruct (Nat.eqb Y X) eqn:E.
      + constructor.
      + apply Hsig; exact YinD. }
    rewrite (ty_subst_env_open_ty_rec sigma' 0 (Ty_FVar X) T0 Hlc') in IHpre.
    assert (HsigmaX : ty_subst_env sigma' (Ty_FVar X) = Ty_FVar X).
    { unfold sigma'. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. }
    rewrite HsigmaX in IHpre.
    assert (Hag : ty_subst_env sigma' T0 = ty_subst_env sigma T0).
    { apply ty_subst_env_agree. intros Y HY.
      unfold sigma'. destruct (Nat.eqb Y X) eqn:E.
      + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst Y. contradiction (HXT HY).
      + reflexivity. }
    rewrite Hag in IHpre.
    exact (lc_ty_open_fvar_rev (ty_subst_env sigma T0) 0 X IHpre).
Qed.
```

```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma X)) ->
  lc_tm (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t).

has_type_lc compiles. Now the big part: the logical relation and its lemmas, then the fundamental theorem.

Let's define:
- Rel, rel_env list, value_rel, env_value_rel.
- rel_val Fixpoint.
- erel Definition.

Then lemmas:
1. rel_val_value.
2. rel_val_ext (env/rho extensionality).
3. lc_env_irrel.
4. rel_val_open_ty (semantic substitution).
5. erel lemmas and multi congruence.
6. fundamental theorem.
7. target.

Let's write the definitions and first lemmas.

```
Definition Rel := tm -> tm -> Prop.
Definition rel_env := list Rel.

Definition value_rel (R : Rel) : Prop :=
  forall t1 t2, R t1 t2 -> value t1 /\ value t2.

Definition env_value_rel (benv : rel_env) : Prop :=
  forall i, value_rel (nth i benv (fun _ _ => False)).

Fixpoint rel_val (T : ty) (benv : rel_env) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => nth i benv (fun _ _ => False)
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t1 t2 =>
      value t1 /\ value t2 /\
      forall a1 a2, rel_val T1 benv rho a1 a2 ->
        exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\
          rel_val T2 benv rho v1 v2
  | Ty_All T1 => fun t1 t2 =>
      value t1 /\ value t2 /\
      forall (U1 U2 : ty) (R : Rel),
        locally_closed_ty U1 -> locally_closed_ty U2 -> value_rel R ->
        exists v1 v2, tm_tapp t1 U1 -->* v1 /\ tm_tapp t2 U2 -->* v2 /\
          rel_val T1 (R :: benv) rho v1 v2
  end.

Definition erel (T : ty) (benv : rel_env) (rho : atom -> Rel) : Rel :=
  fun t1 t2 => exists v1 v2,
    t1 -->* v1 /\ t2 -->* v2 /\ rel_val T benv rho v1 v2.
```
Note: `Definition erel ... : Rel := fun t1 t2 => ...`.

Lemmas:
- `nth_nil`, `nth_cons_0`, `nth_cons_S` by simpl.
- env_value_rel_nil: env_value_rel nil.
- env_value_rel_cons: value_rel R -> env_value_rel benv -> env_value_rel (R::benv).
  Proof: intros HR Hb i; destruct i; simpl; auto.
- env_value_rel_weaken? maybe not.

rel_val_value:
```
Lemma rel_val_value : forall T benv rho,
  env_value_rel benv ->
  (forall X, value_rel (rho X)) ->
  forall t1 t2, rel_val T benv rho t1 t2 -> value t1 /\ value t2.
Proof.
  induction T as [...]; intros benv rho Hb Hr t1 t2 Hrel; simpl in Hrel.
  - apply Hb.  (* BVar: Hrel : nth i benv default t1 t2 *)
  - apply Hr.
  - destruct Hrel as [H1 [H2 H3]]; auto.
  - destruct Hrel as [H1 [H2 H3]]; auto.
Qed.
```
For Arrow/All, Hrel : value t1 /\ value t2 /\ ...; destruct; auto.
For BVar: `exact (Hb i t1 t2 Hrel)`? Hb i : value_rel (nth i benv default) = forall t1 t2, ... -> value t1 /\ value t2. So `apply (Hb i); exact Hrel`.
For FVar: `apply (Hr X); exact Hrel`.
Need `induction T` with benv/rho/t1/t2/Hrel; the statement has forall benv rho ... after T? Arrange:
```
intros T. induction T as [...]; intros benv rho Hb Hr t1 t2 Hrel; simpl in Hrel.
```
The IH for T1: forall benv rho, env_value_rel benv -> (forall X, value_rel (rho X)) -> forall t1 t2, rel_val T1 benv rho t1 t2 -> value t1 /\ value t2. We don't need it actually for Arrow: Hrel has value conjuncts directly. So no recursion needed except BVar/FVar; the proof is non-recursive! Actually for Arrow/All, the value conjuncts are right there; for BVar/FVar use env/rho. So just destruct T; no induction needed:
```
intros T benv rho Hb Hr t1 t2 Hrel.
destruct T; simpl in *.
- apply (Hb n); exact Hrel.
- apply (Hr a); exact Hrel.
- destruct Hrel as [H1 [H2 H3]]; auto.
- destruct Hrel as [H1 [H2 H3]]; auto.
```
Qed. But careful: destruct T with the Fixpoint; simpl in Hrel unfolds. ✓.

- rel_val_env_ext and rho ext. Let's state a combined lemma:
```
Lemma rel_val_ext : forall T benv1 benv2 rho1 rho2,
  (forall i t1 t2, benv1 i t1 t2 <-> benv2 i t1 t2) ->
  (forall X, In X (free_ty_vars_ty T) -> forall t1 t2, rho1 X t1 t2 <-> rho2 X t1 t2) ->
  forall t1 t2, rel_val T benv1 rho1 t1 t2 <-> rel_val T benv2 rho2 t1 t2.
```
The env comparison is pointwise on `benv i`, but benv is a list; `benv1 i` is not a function—benv1 is a list, so `forall i t1 t2, nth i benv1 default t1 t2 <-> nth i benv2 default t1 t2`. Better define:
```
Definition env_equiv (b1 b2 : rel_env) : Prop :=
  forall i t1 t2, nth i b1 (fun _ _ => False) t1 t2 <-> nth i b2 (fun _ _ => False) t1 t2.
Definition rho_equiv_on (T : ty) (r1 r2 : atom -> Rel) : Prop :=
  forall X, In X (free_ty_vars_ty T) -> forall t1 t2, r1 X t1 t2 <-> r2 X t1 t2.
```
Then rel_val_ext statement with env_equiv b1 b2 and rho_equiv_on T r1 r2.

Proof by induction on T:
```
intros T. induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T1 IH];
  intros b1 b2 r1 r2 He Hr t1 t2; simpl.
- apply He.
- apply Hr; simpl; auto.
- split; intros [Hv1 [Hv2 Hf]]; repeat split; auto;
   intros a1 a2 Ha;
   [ assert (Ha' : rel_val T1 b1 r1 a1 a2) by (apply (proj1 (IH1 ... a1 a2)); auto)
   | ... ];
   destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha') as (v1 & v2 & Hs1 & Hs2 & Hrel);
   exists v1,v2; repeat split; auto;
   apply (proj1 (IH2 ... v1 v2)); auto.
```
Here IH1 : forall b1 b2 r1 r2, env_equiv b1 b2 -> rho_equiv_on T1 r1 r2 -> forall t1 t2, rel_val T1 b1 r1 t1 t2 <-> rel_val T1 b2 r2 t1 t2. Wait after intros b1 b2 r1 r2... no: the statement is `forall T b1 b2 r1 r2, ...`; we do `intros T; induction T; intros b1 b2 r1 r2 He Hr t1 t2`. The IH for T1 is `forall b1 b2 r1 r2, env_equiv b1 b2 -> rho_equiv_on T1 r1 r2 -> forall t1 t2, ...`. We need rho_equiv_on T1 r1 r2 from rho_equiv_on (Arrow T1 T2) r1 r2: free vars T1 ⊆ free vars (Arrow T1 T2) = free T1 ++ free T2. Prove by intros X HX; apply Hr; apply in_app_iff; left; auto. ✓.
Similarly for T2.
- All: split; intros [Hv1 [Hv2 Hf]]; repeat split; auto; intros U1 U2 R Hlc1 Hlc2 HR.
  Need env_equiv (R::b1) (R::b2): intro i; destruct i; simpl; [reflexivity | apply He].
  Need rho_equiv_on T1 r1 r2: free vars T1 ⊆ free vars (All T1) = free T1; auto.
  Then use IH1 with these; apply Hf; or from Hf get exists; convert with IH1.
```
For the Arrow case, the exists parts in both directions; the conversion uses IH2. Let's write a helper lemma to reduce repetition:
```
Lemma arrow_rel_ext : forall (A A' B B' : Rel),
  (forall t1 t2, A t1 t2 <-> A' t1 t2) ->
  (forall t1 t2, B t1 t2 <-> B' t1 t2) ->
  forall t1 t2,
   (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall a1 a2, A a1 a2 ->
      exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ B v1 v2)
   <->
   (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall a1 a2, A' a1 a2 ->
      exists v1 v2, tm_app t1 a1 -->* v1 /\ tm_app t2 a2 -->* v2 /\ B' v1 v2).
Proof.
  intros A A' B B' HA HB t1 t2. split; intros [Hv1 [Hv2 Hf]]; repeat split; auto;
    intros a1 a2 Ha.
  - assert (Ha' : A a1 a2) by (apply (proj1 (HA a1 a2)); auto).
    destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha') as (v1 & v2 & H1 & H2 & HBv).
    exists v1, v2. repeat split; auto. apply (proj1 (HB v1 v2)); auto.
  - assert (Ha' : A' a1 a2) by (apply (proj2 (HA a1 a2)); auto).
    destruct (Hf a1 a2 Ha') as (v1 & v2 & H1 & H2 & HBv).
    exists v1, v2. repeat split; auto. apply (proj2 (HB v1 v2)); auto.
Qed.
```
Even simpler: hypothetically, we don't need a separate lemma; but the above is fine.

Similarly all_rel_ext:

Lemma all_rel_ext : forall (A A' : Rel),
  (forall t1 t2, A t1 t2 <-> A' t1 t2) ->

   <->
   (value t1 /\ value t2 /\ forall U1 U2 R, lc U1 -> lc U2 -> value_rel R ->
      exists v1 v2, tapp t1 U1 -->* v1 /\ tapp t2 U2 -->* v2 /\ A' v1 v2).
Proof. ... Qed.
```
Here `lc U` = locally_closed_ty U. Use the same pattern.

- The semantic substitution lemma `rel_val_open_ty`:
Statement:
```
Lemma rel_val_open_ty : forall T U k benv rho,
  lc_ty U ->

Qed.

Multi lemmas:
```
Lemma multi_trans : forall t1 t2 t3, t1 -->* t2 -> t2 -->* t3 -> t1 -->* t3.
Proof. intros t1 t2 t3 H. induction H; intros H2; [exact H2 | eapply multi_step; [eassumption|apply IH; exact H2]]. Qed.
```
Actually induction H: multi_refl: goal t1 -->* t3 given t1 -->* t3 exact. multi_step x y z Hxy Hyz IH: need x -->* t3; apply multi_step with y; [exact Hxy | apply IH; exact H2]. ✓.

```
Lemma multi_app_l : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 -->* t1' -> lc_tm t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 -->* tm_app t1' t2.
Proof. intros t1 t1' t2 H. induction H; intros Hlc.
 - apply multi_refl.
 - eapply multi_step. apply ST_App1; eauto. apply IH; exact Hlc.
Qed.
```
But ST_App1 requires `locally_closed_tm t2`, which is lc_tm t2. ✓. The multi_step constructor: multi_step x y z : x-->y -> y-->*z -> x-->*z. eapply multi_step with (y := tm_app y t2)? The step is app x t2 --> app y t2. So `eapply multi_step; [apply ST_App1; eauto | apply IH; exact Hlc]`. 
Actually `multi_step` has x y z implicit; use `eapply multi_step with (y := tm_app y t2)`.
```
Lemma multi_app_r : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 -->* t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 -->* tm_app v1 t2'.
Proof. intros v1 t2 t2' Hv H. induction H.
 - apply multi_refl.
 - eapply multi_step with (y := tm_app v1 y). apply ST_App2; eauto. apply IH.
Qed.
```
Need careful variable names from induction.
```
Lemma multi_tapp : forall t t' U, t -->* t' -> lc_ty U -> tm_tapp t U -->* tm_tapp t' U.

We'll use these to build erel from rel_val with prefix steps.

A general erel "prepend step" lemma:
```
Lemma erel_step_l : forall T benv rho t1 t1' t2,
  t1 --> t1' -> erel T benv rho t1' t2 -> erel T benv rho t1 t2.
Proof. intros. destruct H0 as (v1&v2&H1&H2&Hr). exists v1,v2. split; [|split]; auto. eapply multi_step; eauto. Qed.
```
and erel_step_r similarly, and erel_multi_l/r (prepend a multi).
```
Lemma erel_multi_l : t1 -->* t1' -> erel T ... t1' t2 -> erel T ... t1 t2.
Proof: destruct erel; exists; multi_trans.
```
Then in T_TAbs: from IH's erel (open body U1) (open body U2), build erel (tapp tabs U1) (tapp tabs U2): first step tapp tabs U1 --> open body U1, so erel_multi_l with multi_tapp_step. Side2 similarly.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let's write it as:
```
Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall benv rho sigma1 sigma2 eta1 eta2,
    env_value_rel benv ->
    (forall X, value_rel (rho X)) ->
    (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma1 X)) ->
    (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma2 X)) ->
    (forall y, lc_tm (eta1 y)) ->
    (forall y, lc_tm (eta2 y)) ->
    (forall y S, In (y,S) Gamma ->
       rel_val S benv rho (eta1 y) (eta2 y)) ->
    erel T benv rho (inst sigma1 eta1 t) (inst sigma2 eta2 t).
```
Proof by induction on H.

T_Var: erel T (eta1 x)(eta2 x). Env gives rel_val T. Need values: rel_val_value with env_value_rel benv, rho value_rel, but the env's entry has GVal? env_value_rel applies to nth of benv, fine. Value of eta terms: rel_val_value on T with benv. Then erel via multi_refl.
```
- (* T_Var *) 
  specialize (Henv x T0 Hin_lookup)? 
```
The env hypothesis is `Henv : forall y S, In (y,S) Gamma0 -> rel_val S benv rho (eta1 y) (eta2 y)`. Need In (x,T0) Gamma0 from Hlook : lookup_context x Gamma0 = Some T0. Prove a lemma:
```
Lemma lookup_context_in : forall x Gamma T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> In (x,T) Gamma.
Proof. induction Gamma as [| [y S] Gamma IH]; simpl; intros H.
 - discriminate.
 - destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
   + inversion H; subst; left; auto.
   + right; apply IH; auto.
Qed.
```
Then `pose proof (Henv x T0 (lookup_context_in _ _ _ Hlook)) as Hrel.` Then `apply rel_val_value` to get values; construct erel:
```
exists (eta1 x), (eta2 x); split; [apply multi_refl|split; [apply multi_refl|exact Hrel]].
```
multi_refl gives eta1 x -->* eta1 x ✓.

T_Abs: as planned. Need careful handling of `inst` unfolding: inst sigma eta (tm_abs T1 t2) = tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (tm_subst_env eta (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2))? Let's compute: tm_ty_subst_env sigma (tm_abs T1 t2) = tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2). Then tm_subst_env eta of that = tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (tm_subst_env eta (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2)). And inst sigma eta t2 = tm_subst_env eta (tm_ty_subst_env sigma t2) (by definition). So inst (abs) = tm_abs (ty_subst_env sigma T1) (inst sigma eta t2). Need a rewrite; `unfold inst; simpl.` should compute.

Let's define has_type_inst_lc:
```
Lemma has_type_inst_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma eta,
    (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty (sigma X)) ->
    (forall y, lc_tm (eta y)) ->
    lc_tm (inst sigma eta t).
Proof.
  intros. unfold inst.
  apply lc_tm_subst_env; auto.
  apply has_type_lc; auto.
Qed.
```

Let's create helper lemmas:
```
Lemma eta_update_self : forall x a eta, (fun y => if y =? x then a else eta y) x = a.
Proof. intros. simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.
Lemma eta_update_other : forall x a eta y, y <> x -> (fun z => if z =? x then a else eta z) y = eta y.
Proof. intros. simpl. destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E. apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. contradiction. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Note the `simpl` may be needed to beta-reduce.

Define `insert_at k R l` = l[0..k-1] ++ [R] ++ l[k..]. Then:
- For BVar i: open_ty_rec k gives if i=k then U else BVar i (indices unchanged). LHS rel_val U benv (relation of U with env benv); RHS rel_val (BVar i) (insert_at k RU benv) = nth i (insert ...). The issue is that open_ty doesn't decrement indices, so the opened type's index i refers to the same binder as T's index i, but the env benv has shifted positions. But after removing the k-th binder, positions shift; however open_ty does NOT shift indices, so the opened type's index i>k would refer to the (i+1)-th original binder if we think of the removed binder—creating a bug in open_ty's scoping. Since the language's open_ty is defined without decrementing, well-formed usage requires no indices >k. So lc_ty_at (S k) T is genuinely needed.

Lemma lc_ty_open:
```
Lemma lc_ty_open : forall T U k,
  lc_ty_at (S k) T -> lc_ty U -> lc_ty_at k (open_ty_rec k U T).
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [i|X|T1 IH1 T2 IH2|T1 IH]; intros U k HT HU; simpl in *.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst i.
      apply (lc_ty_at_mono 0 k U); [lia | exact HU].   (* U lc at 0 -> at k *)
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. constructor. inversion HT. lia.
  - constructor.
  - inversion HT. constructor; auto.
  - inversion HT. constructor. apply IH with (k := S k); auto.
Qed.
```
Check BVar true: HT : lc_ty_at (S k) (BVar k) gives k < S k ✓; goal lc_ty_at k U; from HU : lc_ty_at 0 U mono. ✓. False: HT gives i < S k. E : i≠k. Need i<k? From i<S k means i≤k; i≠k → i<k. ✓.
Arrow: inversion HT gives lc_ty_at (S k) T1 and T2; IH1 with k, IH2. ✓.
All: HT : lc_ty_at (S k) (All T1) = lc_ty_at (S (S k)) T1; open gives All (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1); goal lc_ty_at k (All ...) = lc_ty_at (S k) (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1); IH with k:=S k. ✓.

has_type_lc_ty:
```
Lemma has_type_lc_ty : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T -> lc_ty T.
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [Delta0 Gamma0 x T0 Hlook Hwf
                 | L Delta0 Gamma0 T1 t2 T2 Hwf Hpre IHpre
                 | Delta0 Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                 | L Delta0 Gamma0 t T0 Hpre IHpre
                 | Delta0 Gamma0 t T U Ht IHt HwfU
                 | Delta0 Gamma0 t1 t2 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2].
  - apply wf_ty_lc; exact Hwf.
  - unfold lc_ty. constructor.
    + apply wf_ty_lc; exact Hwf.
    + apply IHpre? Wait IHpre for the T_Abs premise: the premise is forall x, ~In x L -> has_type Delta0 (x|->T1;Gamma0) (open_tm t2 FVarx) T2. The IHpre likely : forall x, ~In x L -> lc_ty T2? Since the motive is lc_ty T (ignoring Delta,Gamma,t), the IH for the premise might be `forall x Hnot, lc_ty T2` (the property doesn't depend on x/t). So `specialize (IHpre (fresh_atom L) ...)`; or since it's a forall, maybe `apply IHpre; apply fresh_atom_notin`. Let's write:
      specialize (IHpre (fresh_atom L)); apply IHpre; apply fresh_atom_notin.
      Actually IHpre : forall x, ~In x L -> lc_ty T2? Then `exact (IHpre (fresh_atom L) (fresh_atom_notin L))`.
  - (* App: IH1 : lc_ty (Arrow T1 T2); need lc_ty T2 *) 
    pose proof (lc_ty_at_arrow_inv 0 T1 T2 IH1) as [_ H2]; exact H2.
  - (* TAbs: IHpre : forall X, ~In X L -> lc_ty (open_ty T0 FVarX)? motive lc_ty T where T = open_ty T0 FVarX. Need lc_ty (All T0). *)
    unfold lc_ty. apply lc_ty_all.
    specialize (IHpre (fresh_atom L) (fresh_atom_notin L)).
    unfold lc_ty, open_ty in IHpre.
    exact (lc_ty_open_fvar_rev T0 0 (fresh_atom L) IHpre).
  - (* TApp: IHt : lc_ty (All T) -> lc_ty_at 1 T. Need lc_ty (open_ty T U). *)
    pose proof (lc_ty_at_all_inv 0 T IHt) as HT.
    unfold lc_ty in *. 
    exact (lc_ty_open T U 0 HT (wf_ty_lc _ _ HwfU)).
    (* lc_ty_open T U 0 : lc_ty_at 1 T -> lc_ty U -> lc_ty_at 0 (open_ty_rec 0 U T) *)
  - (* choice *) exact IH1? both branches same type T0; IH1 : lc_ty T0; exact IH1.
Qed.
```

Lemma has_type_inst_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,

  forall sigma eta,

  intros. unfold inst.

  apply has_type_lc; auto.

Then helper lemmas for eta updates:
```
Lemma eta_update_self : forall x a eta, (fun y => if y =? x then a else eta y) x = a.
Proof. intros. simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.
Lemma eta_update_other : forall x a eta y, y <> x -> (fun z => if z =? x then a else eta z) y = eta y.
Proof. intros. simpl. destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E. apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; contradiction. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Note `simpl` beta-reduces the application of the lambda to y/x; then `if y=?x`. For eta_update_self, simpl gives if x=?x; rewrite eqb_refl; reflexivity.

      assert (Hb : tm_subst_env eta1' (tm_ty_subst_env sigma1 t2) = b1).
      { unfold b1. apply tm_subst_env_agree. intros y Hy.
        apply (eta_update_other x a1 eta1 y). intro Heq; subst y.
        apply Hxfree. apply free_tm_vars_ty_subst. exact Hy. }
      rewrite Hb. reflexivity. }
```
